Q1. A team completes a job in 8.57 actual working days. There is a 3-day stoppage. In how many calendar days is the job completed?
A. 17.57 days
B. 10.57 days
C. 9.26 days
D. 11.57 days
Answer: 11.57 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_interrupted_work
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
T_{calendar}=T_{active}+T_{idle}
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
T_{active}=\frac{180}{12+9}
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=T_{active}+3=11.57
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=t_a+t_i
\]
Answer: 11.57 days

---

Q2. For the same work, A and B have capacities 20:30. What is the ratio of their times?
A. 20:30
B. 30:50
C. 3:2
D. 50:30
Answer: 3:2
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_time_ratio_from_efficiency
Explanation:
Set up the rate relation for this case.
\[
T_A:T_B=E_B:E_A
\]
Put the given values into that relation.
\[
T_A:T_B=30:20
\]
Solve for the asked value.
\[
T_A:T_B=3:2
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=3:2
\]
Answer: 3:2

---

Q3. A road crew lays 18 metres of road per day, while rain damages 5 metres per day. If 180 metres must be completed, in how many days will the road be finished?
A. 16.85 days
B. 9.85 days
C. 20.77 days
D. 13.85 days
Answer: 13.85 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_digging_filling_negative_work
Explanation:
Write output as rate multiplied by active time.
\[
T=\frac{W}{r_+-r_-}
\]
Add the outputs of all active agents.
\[
T=\frac{180}{18-5}
\]
Simplify the total output.
\[
T=13.85
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{W}{r_n}
\]
Answer: 13.85 days

---

Q4. The working capacity ratio A:B is 4:7, while B:C is 7:8. Find the ratio A:B:C?
A. 8:7:4
B. 4:7:8
C. 4:7:8:2
D. 4:7:8:3
Answer: 4:7:8
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_efficiency_chain
Explanation:
Set up the rate relation for this case.
\[
B_c
\]
Put the given values into that relation.
\[
LCM(7,7)=7
\]
Solve for the asked value.
\[
A:B:C=4:7:8
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
A:B:C=4:7:8
\]
Answer: 4:7:8

---

Q5. For equal working time, A is paid ₹450 and B is paid ₹600. What is the ratio of their work?
A. 45:30
B. 1050:600
C. 3:4
D. 600:450
Answer: 3:4
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_efficiency_from_wages
Explanation:
Set up the rate relation for this case.
\[
W_A:W_B=E_A:E_B
\]
Put the given values into that relation.
\[
E_A:E_B=450:600
\]
Solve for the asked value.
\[
E_A:E_B=3:4
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
E=3:4
\]
Answer: 3:4

Q6. One man completes 4 items in a day and one woman completes 3 items in a day. If 3 men and 4 women work together, in how many days will they complete 360 items?
A. 15 days
B. 19 days
C. 12 days
D. 16 days
Answer: 15 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_team_a_vs_team_b
Explanation:
Set up the rate relation for this case.
\[
T=\frac{W}{\sum ne}
\]
Put the given values into that relation.
\[
T=\frac{360}{3\times 4+4\times 3}
\]
Solve for the asked value.
\[
T=15
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=W/R
\]
Answer: 15 days

---

Q7. A tap gives 12 litres per minute. How much water will it supply in 40 minutes?
A. 485 litres
B. 480 litres
C. 478 litres
D. 576 litres
Answer: 480 litres
Difficulty: Easy
Family: pc_tank_capacity_from_rate
Explanation:
Write filling rates as positive and emptying rates as negative.
\[
C=rt
\]
Find the effective tank rate.
\[
C=12\times 40
\]
Divide the required tank work by that rate.
\[
C=480
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
C=r\times t
\]
Answer: 480 litres

---

Q8. A printing job produces 100 pages each hour for 6 hours. With 90% acceptance, what is the accepted page count?
A. 540 pages
B. 539 pages
C. 810 pages
D. 544 pages
Answer: 540 pages
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_work_quality_rejection
Explanation:
Convert each person's work into contribution units.
\[
O_a=O\times \frac{p}{100}
\]
Use contribution ratio for the share.
\[
O_a=100\times 6\times \frac{90}{100}
\]
Apply the wage or contract amount.
\[
O_a=540
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
O_a=gross\times p/100
\]
Answer: 540 pages

---

Q9. Normally a pipe fills a tank in 10 hours, but with leakage it fills in 15 hours. What is the emptying time of the leak?
A. 30 hours
B. 37 1/2 hours
C. 31 hours
D. 36 hours
Answer: 30 hours
Difficulty: Medium
Family: pc_leak_hidden_rate
Explanation:
Write filling rates as positive and emptying rates as negative.
\[
\frac{1}{L}=\frac{1}{F}-\frac{1}{T}
\]
Find the effective tank rate.
\[
\frac{1}{L}=\frac{1}{10}-\frac{1}{15}
\]
Divide the required tank work by that rate.
\[
L=30
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
L=\frac{FT}{T-F}
\]
Answer: 30 hours

---

Q10. A group of 30 people has food for 24 days. They consume it for 8 days, and then the group becomes 40. How many days will the remaining food last?
A. 11 days
B. 9 days
C. 17 days
D. 12 days
Answer: 12 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_food_remaining_stock
Explanation:
Convert the stock into consumption units.
\[
D=\frac{S-consumed}{people}
\]
Subtract the stock already consumed.
\[
D=\frac{720-30\times 8}{40}
\]
Divide the remaining stock by the new daily use.
\[
D=12
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
D=\frac{S_l}{P_2}
\]
Answer: 12 days

---

Q11. A finishes a job in 20 days and B in 30 days. What is A:B work ratio over the same duration?
A. 50:30
B. 30:50
C. 3:2
D. 20:30
Answer: 3:2
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_work_done_ratio_from_times
Explanation:
Set up the rate relation for this case.
\[
W_A:W_B=\frac{1}{20}:\frac{1}{30}
\]
Put the given values into that relation.
\[
W_A:W_B=30:20
\]
Solve for the asked value.
\[
W_A:W_B=3:2
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
E=3:2
\]
Answer: 3:2

---

Q12. A and B work on a job but leave before it is completed. A leaves 2 days early and B leaves 3 days early. If their individual times are 6 and 30 days, how long does the job take?
A. 5.17 days
B. 5.38 days
C. 2.17 days
D. 7.17 days
Answer: 7.17 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_backward_leave
Explanation:
Set up the rate relation for this case.
\[
r_A(T-a)+r_B(T-b)=W
\]
Put the given values into that relation.
\[
30(T-2)+6(T-3)=180
\]
Solve for the asked value.
\[
T=7.17
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{W+A\times 2+B\times 3}{A+B}
\]
Answer: 7.17 days

---

Q13. There are 720 ration packets in a camp. If 24 packets are used each day, how many days will they last?
A. 37 1/2 days
B. 32 days
C. 30 days
D. 25 days
Answer: 30 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_resource_rate_equivalence
Explanation:
Convert the stock into consumption units.
\[
D=\frac{S}{R}
\]
Subtract the stock already consumed.
\[
D=\frac{720}{24}
\]
Divide the remaining stock by the new daily use.
\[
D=30
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
D=\frac{S}{R}
\]
Answer: 30 days

Q14. For the same work, AB finishes in 10 days, BC in 12 days and CA in 15 days. Find A's time in days?
A. 29 days
B. 26 days
C. 18 days
D. 24 days
Answer: 24 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_pairwise_ab_bc_ac
Explanation:
Set up the rate relation for this case.
\[
2(A+B+C)=(A+B)+(B+C)+(C+A)
\]
Put the given values into that relation.
\[
AB=10,\ BC=12,\ CA=15
\]
Solve for the asked value.
\[
T_A=24
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
A=\frac{AB+AC-BC}{2}
\]
Answer: 24 days

---

Q15. A production batch uses three machines with hourly item counts 12, 15 and 18. What is the number of items made in 6 hours?
A. 274 items
B. 270 items
C. 271 items
D. 216 items
Answer: 270 items
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_machine_breakdown_midway
Explanation:
Write output as rate multiplied by active time.
\[
O=\sum r_it_i
\]
Add the outputs of all active agents.
\[
O=12\times 6+15\times 6+18\times 6
\]
Simplify the total output.
\[
O=270
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
O=(r_1+r_2+r_3)t
\]
Answer: 270 items

---

Q18. If a work is completed in 30 days and 6 days have passed, what fraction of the work is yet to be done?
A. 1/5
B. 5/4
C. 4/5
D. 4/1
Answer: 4/5
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_basic_residual_work
Explanation:
Set up the rate relation for this case.
\[
R=1-\frac{t}{T}
\]
Put the given values into that relation.
\[
R=1-\frac{6}{30}
\]
Solve for the asked value.
\[
R=\frac{24}{30}=4/5
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
R=\frac{30-6}{30}=4/5
\]
Answer: 4/5

Q19. Pipe A is an inlet of 12 hours and pipe B is an outlet of 36 hours. If both are opened together, how many hours will the tank take to fill?
A. 23 hours
B. 18 hours
C. 13 1/2 hours
D. 20 hours
Answer: 18 hours
Difficulty: Medium
Family: pc_basic_fill_empty
Explanation:
Write filling rates as positive and emptying rates as negative.
\[
T=\frac{W_l}{r_f-r_e}
\]
Find the effective tank rate.
\[
r=1/12-1/36
\]
Divide the required tank work by that rate.
\[
T=18
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{W_l}{r_n}
\]
Answer: 18 hours

Q20. Two filling pipes A and B take 12 and 18 hours respectively. If pipe A is closed after 2 hours, what is the time in hours to fill the tank?
A. 7 1/2 hours
B. 12 hours
C. 21 hours
D. 15 hours
Answer: 15 hours
Difficulty: Medium
Family: pc_pipe_closed_before_completion
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
W_1=(r_A+r_B)t_1,\quad T=t_1+\frac{W-W_1}{r_B}
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
W_1=(3+2)\times 2
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=2+\frac{26}{2}=15
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=t_1+\frac{W-W_1}{r_B}
\]
Answer: 15 hours

---

Q21. A team has completed 36 documents from a total of 120. The last stage completes 6 documents per day. How many days will that stage take?
A. 11 1/5 days
B. 15 days

D. 14 days
Answer: 14 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_phase_state_reconstruction

\[
x=\frac{W-W_1}{r}
\]
Put the given values into that relation.
\[
x=\frac{120-36}{6}
\]
Solve for the asked value.
\[
x=14
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
x=\frac{W_l}{r}
\]
Answer: 14 days

Q22. A and B start a work together. A can complete it in 18 days and B in 6 days. After 3 days, A leaves. In how many days from the start will the work be completed?
A. 7 1/2 days
B. 1 day
C. 5 days
D. 6 days
Answer: 5 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_forward_leave
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
W=(r_A+r_B)t_1+r_Bt_2
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
W_1=(5+15)\times 3
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=3+\frac{30}{15}=5
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=3+\frac{W-W_1}{B}
\]
Answer: 5 days

---

Q23. 100 of 300 forms are complete. 10 workers are available and 10 days remain. How many additional workers are required?
A. 9 workers
B. 15 workers
C. 14 workers
D. 10 workers
Answer: 10 workers
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_worker_added_to_meet_deadline
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
N_{extra}=\left\lceil\frac{R}{r\times t}\right\rceil-N_c
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
N_{extra}=\left\lceil\frac{200}{1\times 10}\right\rceil-10
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
N_{extra}=10
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
N_{extra}=N_r-N_c
\]
Answer: 10 workers

---

Q24. Two workers take turns, with A starting. A handles 8 files per turn and B handles 4 files per turn. In how many days will 72 files be finished?
A. 16 days
B. 12 days
C. 10 days
D. 9 3/5 days
Answer: 12 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_alternating_days_two_workers
Explanation:
Find the work completed in one full cycle.
\[
W_{cycle}=\sum r_i
\]
Count full cycles before the last turn.
\[
W_{cycle}=8+4
\]
Add only the needed part of the final turn.
\[
T=12
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=cycles+final\ turn
\]
Answer: 12 days

---

Q26. A tap fills 12 litres in one hour and a drain removes 5 litres in the next hour. This pattern repeats. If the tank capacity is 60 litres, in how many hours will it be full?
A. 12 1/4 hours
B. 11 1/3 hours
C. 16 1/3 hours
D. 19 1/3 hours
Answer: 16 1/3 hours
Difficulty: Hard
Family: pc_alternating_fill_empty
Explanation:
Write filling rates as positive and emptying rates as negative.
\[
W_{cycle}=12-5
\]
Find the effective tank rate.
\[
60\ units,\ cycle=7
\]
Divide the required tank work by that rate.
\[
T=16\frac{1}{3}
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=C_f+t_f
\]
Answer: 16 1/3 hours

Q29. A food stock lasts 36 days for 30 people. How many days will it last for 25 people?
A. 43 1/5 days
B. 42 1/5 days
C. 47 1/5 days
D. 64 4/5 days
Answer: 43 1/5 days
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_food_resource_basic
Explanation:
Convert the stock into consumption units.
\[
M_1D_1=M_2D_2
\]
Subtract the stock already consumed.
\[
30\times 36=25\times D_2
\]
Divide the remaining stock by the new daily use.
\[
D_2=43.2
\]

\[
D_2=\frac{30\times 36}{25}
\]
Answer: 43 1/5 days

Q31. A and B can work in the ratio 4:5. They complete the job together in 10 days. What is A's time for the same job?
A. 12 1/2 days
B. 22 1/2 days
C. 8 days
D. 19 days
Answer: 22 1/2 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_relative_efficiency_percent
Explanation:
Set up the rate relation for this case.
\[
W=(a+b)T
\]
Put the given values into that relation.
\[
W=(4+5)\times 10
\]
Solve for the asked value.
\[
T_A=\frac{90}{4}=22.5
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T_A=\frac{(a+b)T}{a}=\frac{(4+5)10}{4}=22.5
\]
Answer: 22 1/2 days

Q38. A tank receives water for 30 minutes at 15 litres per minute. What is the quantity of water received?

Explanation:
Write filling rates as positive and emptying rates as negative.
\[
C=rt
\]

C=15\times 30

\]

Subtract the work already completed.
\[
W_1=38\times 5
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=5+\frac{170}{33}=10.15
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=t_1+\frac{W-W_1}{r_2}
\]
Answer: 10.15 days

Q86. One worker can do a job in 8 days and another can do it in 6 days. How long will they take together?
A. 14 days
B. 3.43 days
C. 6 days
D. 7 days
Answer: 3.43 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_lcm_hidden_total_work
Explanation:
Take the LCM as total work.
\[
W=LCM(8,6)=24
\]
Find each worker's one-period efficiency.
\[
A=24/8=3,\quad B=24/6=4
\]
Divide total work by the combined efficiency.
\[
T=\frac{24}{3+4}=3.43
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{8\times 6}{8+6}=3.43
\]
Answer: 3.43 days

\[

\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=T_{active}+3=7.44
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=t_a+t_i
\]
Answer: 7.44 days

Q119. In 8 hours, a printer works at 120 pages per hour. If 90% are approved, how many pages are approved?

\]

\[
O_a=120\times 8\times \frac{90}{100}
\]

\[

\[

\]

Q120. Normally a pipe fills a tank in 15 hours, but with leakage it fills in 20 hours. What is the emptying time of the leak?
A. 61 hours
B. 75 hours
C. 66 hours
D. 60 hours
Answer: 60 hours
Difficulty: Medium
Family: pc_leak_hidden_rate
Explanation:
Write filling rates as positive and emptying rates as negative.
\[
\frac{1}{L}=\frac{1}{F}-\frac{1}{T}
\]
Find the effective tank rate.
\[
\frac{1}{L}=\frac{1}{15}-\frac{1}{20}
\]
Divide the required tank work by that rate.
\[
L=60
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
L=\frac{FT}{T-F}
\]
Answer: 60 hours

\]

Q130. A and B work together at first. A's time is 24 days and B's time is 6 days. If A leaves after 4 days, when will the job be finished from the start?
A. 2 days
B. 7 1/2 days
C. 5 days
D. 9 days
Answer: 5 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_forward_leave
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
W=(r_A+r_B)t_1+r_Bt_2
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
W_1=(5+20)\times 4
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=4+\frac{20}{20}=5
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=4+\frac{W-W_1}{B}
\]
Answer: 5 days

\]

Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_relative_efficiency_percent
Explanation:
Set up the rate relation for this case.
\[
W=(a+b)T
\]
Put the given values into that relation.
\[
W=(2+3)\times 6
\]
Solve for the asked value.
\[
T_A=\frac{30}{2}=15
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T_A=\frac{(a+b)T}{a}=\frac{(2+3)6}{2}=15
\]
Answer: 15 days

Q163. One worker can do a job in 6 days and another can do it in 15 days. How long will they take together?
A. 10 1/2 days
B. 21 days
C. 6 days
D. 4.29 days
Answer: 4.29 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_lcm_hidden_total_work
Explanation:
Take the LCM as total work.
\[
W=LCM(6,15)=30
\]
Find each worker's one-period efficiency.
\[
A=30/6=5,\quad B=30/15=2
\]
Divide total work by the combined efficiency.
\[
T=\frac{30}{5+2}=4.29
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{6\times 15}{6+15}=4.29
\]
Answer: 4.29 days

M_1D_1H_1=M_2D_2H_2
\]
Add the outputs of all active agents.
\[
12\times 10\times 8=M_2\times 16\times 6
\]
Simplify the total output.
\[
M_2=10
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
M_2=\frac{12\times 10\times 8}{16\times 6}=10
\]
Answer: 10 workers

---

Q189. A job needs 4 4/5 days of work. Work stops for 2 days during the job. How many days pass from start to finish?

Q201. If A can complete a work in 30 days and B can complete it in 36 days, how many days are needed when both work at the same time?
A. 33 days
B. 30 days
C. 16.36 days
D. 66 days
Answer: 16.36 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_lcm_hidden_total_work
Explanation:
Take the LCM as total work.
\[
W=LCM(30,36)=180
\]
Find each worker's one-period efficiency.
\[
A=180/30=6,\quad B=180/36=5
\]
Divide total work by the combined efficiency.
\[
T=\frac{180}{6+5}=16.36
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{30\times 36}{30+36}=16.36
\]
Answer: 16.36 days

\[
T=\frac{W}{r_+-r_-}
\]
Add the outputs of all active agents.
\[
T=\frac{150}{15-5}
\]
Simplify the total output.
\[
T=15
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{W}{r_n}
\]
Answer: 15 days

\]

\[

\]

N_{extra}=10

\[

\]
Answer: 10 workers

C. 19 workers
D. 13 workers
Answer: 10 workers
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_man_days_hours_basic
Explanation:
Set up the rate relation for this case.
\[
M_1D_1H_1=M_2D_2H_2
\]
Put the given values into that relation.
\[
12\times 10\times 8=M_2\times 16\times 6
\]
Solve for the asked value.
\[
M_2=10
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
M_2=\frac{12\times 10\times 8}{16\times 6}=10
\]
Answer: 10 workers

---

Q264. 18 workers finish a job in 8 days, working 5 hours daily. How many workers should work 6 hours daily to finish it in 12 days?
A. 10 workers
B. 27 workers
C. 18 workers
D. 13 workers
Answer: 10 workers
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_farm_harvest_workers
Explanation:
Write output as rate multiplied by active time.
\[
M_1D_1H_1=M_2D_2H_2
\]
Add the outputs of all active agents.
\[
18\times 8\times 5=M_2\times 12\times 6
\]
Simplify the total output.
\[
M_2=10
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
M_2=\frac{18\times 8\times 5}{12\times 6}=10
\]
Answer: 10 workers

Q265. A team completes a job in 3 3/4 actual working days. There is a 3-day stoppage. In how many calendar days is the job completed?

B. 8 3/4 days
C. 6 3/4 days
D. 5 2/5 days
Answer: 6 3/4 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_interrupted_work
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
T_{calendar}=T_{active}+T_{idle}
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
T_{active}=\frac{240}{40+24}
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=T_{active}+3=6.75
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=t_a+t_i
\]
Answer: 6 3/4 days

Q270. Normally a pipe fills a tank in 10 hours, but with leakage it fills in 16 hours. What is the emptying time of the leak?
A. 26 2/3 hours
B. 31 2/3 hours
C. 29 2/3 hours
D. 32 hours
Answer: 26 2/3 hours
Difficulty: Medium
Family: pc_leak_hidden_rate
Explanation:
Write filling rates as positive and emptying rates as negative.
\[
\frac{1}{L}=\frac{1}{F}-\frac{1}{T}
\]
Find the effective tank rate.
\[
\frac{1}{L}=\frac{1}{10}-\frac{1}{16}
\]
Divide the required tank work by that rate.
\[
L=26\frac{2}{3}
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
L=\frac{FT}{T-F}
\]
Answer: 26 2/3 hours

Q273. In a joint job, A and B have individual times 12 and 24 days. A is not present in the last 2 days and B in the last 3 days. What is the completion time?

B. 10 1/3 days
C. 11 1/3 days
D. 12.92 days
Answer: 10 1/3 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_backward_leave
Explanation:
Set up the rate relation for this case.
\[
r_A(T-a)+r_B(T-b)=W
\]
Put the given values into that relation.
\[
10(T-2)+5(T-3)=120
\]
Solve for the asked value.
\[
T=10\frac{1}{3}
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{W+A\times 2+B\times 3}{A+B}
\]
Answer: 10 1/3 days

Q277. A and B begin a job together. Their individual times are 12 days and 18 days. A leaves after 4 days. What is the total completion time?
A. 14 days
B. 12 days
C. 18 days
D. 16 days
Answer: 12 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_forward_leave
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
W=(r_A+r_B)t_1+r_Bt_2
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
W_1=(6+4)\times 4
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=4+\frac{32}{4}=12
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=4+\frac{W-W_1}{B}
\]
Answer: 12 days

Q278. 12 workers have done 120 files out of 240. To finish the rest in 8 days, how many additional workers are needed?
A. 0.5 workers
B. 4.5 workers
C. 3 workers
D. 4 workers
Answer: 3 workers
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_worker_added_to_meet_deadline
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
N_{extra}=\left\lceil\frac{R}{r\times t}\right\rceil-N_c
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
N_{extra}=\left\lceil\frac{120}{1\times 8}\right\rceil-12
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
N_{extra}=3
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
N_{extra}=N_r-N_c
\]
Answer: 3 workers

Q279. A and B work on alternate days, starting with A. A completes 6 files in a day and B completes 6 files in a day. If 72 files are to be completed, in how many days will the job be finished?
A. 7 days
B. 13 days
C. 12 days
D. 9 days
Answer: 12 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_alternating_days_two_workers
Explanation:
Find the work completed in one full cycle.
\[
W_{cycle}=\sum r_i
\]
Count full cycles before the last turn.
\[
W_{cycle}=6+6
\]
Add only the needed part of the final turn.
\[
T=12
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=cycles+final\ turn
\]
Answer: 12 days

Q286. One worker can do a job in 30 days and another can do it in 24 days. How long will they take together?
A. 27 days
B. 24 days
C. 54 days
D. 13 1/3 days
Answer: 13 1/3 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_lcm_hidden_total_work
Explanation:
Take the LCM as total work.
\[
W=LCM(30,24)=120
\]
Find each worker's one-period efficiency.
\[
A=120/30=4,\quad B=120/24=5
\]
Divide total work by the combined efficiency.
\[
T=\frac{120}{4+5}=13\frac{1}{3}
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{30\times 24}{30+24}=13\frac{1}{3}
\]
Answer: 13 1/3 days

Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
N_{extra}=\left\lceil\frac{R}{r\times t}\right\rceil-N_c
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
N_{extra}=\left\lceil\frac{240}{1\times 10}\right\rceil-10
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
N_{extra}=14
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
N_{extra}=N_r-N_c
\]
Answer: 14 workers

---

Q307. If A can complete a work in 12 days and B can complete it in 18 days, how many days are needed when both work at the same time?
A. 30 days
B. 15 days
C. 12 days
D. 7 1/5 days
Answer: 7 1/5 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_lcm_hidden_total_work
Explanation:
Take the LCM as total work.
\[
W=LCM(12,18)=36
\]
Find each worker's one-period efficiency.
\[
A=36/12=3,\quad B=36/18=2
\]
Divide total work by the combined efficiency.
\[
T=\frac{36}{3+2}=7.2
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{12\times 18}{12+18}=7.2
\]
Answer: 7 1/5 days

\[
B=\frac{AT}{A-T}
\]
Answer: 13 1/3 hours

Q337. A printing job produces 80 pages each hour for 5 hours. With 90% acceptance, what is the accepted page count?
A. 359 pages
B. 360 pages
C. 356 pages
D. 432 pages
Answer: 360 pages
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_work_quality_rejection
Explanation:
Convert each person's work into contribution units.
\[
O_a=O\times \frac{p}{100}
\]
Use contribution ratio for the share.
\[
O_a=80\times 5\times \frac{90}{100}
\]
Apply the wage or contract amount.
\[
O_a=360
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
O_a=gross\times p/100
\]
Answer: 360 pages

Q338. Without leakage, a tank fills in 12 hours. With leakage, it fills in 22 hours. Find the leak's emptying time in hours?
A. 30 2/5 hours
B. 39 3/5 hours
C. 26 2/5 hours
D. 28 2/5 hours
Answer: 26 2/5 hours
Difficulty: Medium
Family: pc_leak_hidden_rate
Explanation:
Write filling rates as positive and emptying rates as negative.
\[
\frac{1}{L}=\frac{1}{F}-\frac{1}{T}
\]
Find the effective tank rate.
\[
\frac{1}{L}=\frac{1}{12}-\frac{1}{22}
\]
Divide the required tank work by that rate.
\[
L=26.4
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
L=\frac{FT}{T-F}
\]
Answer: 26 2/5 hours

Q339. 24 people have food for 30 days. After 5 days, there are 30 people. For how many additional days will the food be sufficient?

D. 24 days
Answer: 20 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_food_remaining_stock
Explanation:

\[
D=\frac{S-consumed}{people}
\]
Subtract the stock already consumed.
\[
D=\frac{720-24\times 5}{30}
\]
Divide the remaining stock by the new daily use.
\[
D=20
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
D=\frac{S_l}{P_2}
\]
Answer: 20 days

D. 13.54 days
Answer: 8.54 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_backward_leave

\[

\]

\[

\]

\]

\]
Answer: 8.54 days

\]

\]

\]

Q354. A and B can separately complete a job in 18 days and 10 days. What is the time taken when they work together?
A. 14 days
B. 6.43 days
C. 10 days
D. 28 days
Answer: 6.43 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_lcm_hidden_total_work
Explanation:
Take the LCM as total work.
\[
W=LCM(18,10)=90
\]
Find each worker's one-period efficiency.
\[
A=90/18=5,\quad B=90/10=9
\]
Divide total work by the combined efficiency.
\[
T=\frac{90}{5+9}=6.43
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{18\times 10}{18+10}=6.43
\]
Answer: 6.43 days

Answer: 12 1/2 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_conditional_activation
Explanation:
Find the work completed in one full cycle.
\[
W_{cycle}=\sum r_i
\]
Count full cycles before the last turn.
\[
W_{cycle}=8+4+5
\]
Add only the needed part of the final turn.
\[
T=12.5
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=cycles+final\ turn
\]
Answer: 12 1/2 days

Q374. If A can complete a work in 24 days and B can complete it in 10 days, how many days are needed when both work at the same time?
A. 10 days
B. 7.06 days
C. 17 days
D. 34 days
Answer: 7.06 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_lcm_hidden_total_work
Explanation:
Take the LCM as total work.
\[
W=LCM(24,10)=120
\]
Find each worker's one-period efficiency.
\[
A=120/24=5,\quad B=120/10=12
\]
Divide total work by the combined efficiency.
\[
T=\frac{120}{5+12}=7.06
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{24\times 10}{24+10}=7.06
\]
Answer: 7.06 days

C. 11 3/4 hours
D. 10 1/5 hours
Answer: 12 3/4 hours
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_alternating_hours
Explanation:
Find the work completed in one full cycle.
\[
W_{cycle}=\sum r_i
\]
Count full cycles before the last turn.
\[
W_{cycle}=8+6
\]
Add only the needed part of the final turn.
\[
T=12.75
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=cycles+final\ turn
\]
Answer: 12 3/4 hours

Q412. A can do a work in 18 days and B in 10 days. After A works for 2 days alone, both work together. In how many days is the work completed?
A. 3.71 days
B. 9.26 days
C. 7.71 days
D. 6.71 days
Answer: 7.71 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_delayed_join
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
W_1=r_At_1,\ T_2=\frac{W-W_1}{r_A+r_B}
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
W_1=40\times 2,\quad R=720-80
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=2+\frac{640}{40+72}=7.71
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=2+\frac{720-40\times 2}{40+72}
\]
Answer: 7.71 days

Q413. Pipe A is an inlet of 12 hours and pipe B is an outlet of 36 hours. If both are opened together, how many hours will the tank take to fill?
A. 22 hours
B. 24 hours
C. 20 hours
D. 16 hours
Answer: 20 hours
Difficulty: Medium
Family: pc_find_pipe_rate_from_net_time
Explanation:
Write filling rates as positive and emptying rates as negative.
\[
\frac{1}{B}=\frac{1}{T}-\frac{1}{A}
\]
Find the effective tank rate.
\[
\frac{1}{B}=\frac{1}{10}-\frac{1}{20}
\]
Divide the required tank work by that rate.
\[
B=20
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
B=\frac{AT}{A-T}
\]
Answer: 20 hours

Write output as rate multiplied by active time.
\[
T=\frac{W}{r_+-r_-}
\]
Add the outputs of all active agents.
\[
T=\frac{150}{12-4}
\]
Simplify the total output.
\[
T=18.75
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{W}{r_n}
\]
Answer: 18 3/4 days

---

Q451. A and B can separately complete a work in 30 and 36 days. A is absent for the last 2 days and B is absent for the last 3 days. In how many days is the work completed?

B. 19.82 days
C. 13.82 days
D. 18.82 days
Answer: 18.82 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_backward_leave
Explanation:
Set up the rate relation for this case.
\[
r_A(T-a)+r_B(T-b)=W
\]
Put the given values into that relation.
\[
6(T-2)+5(T-3)=180
\]
Solve for the asked value.
\[
T=18.82
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{W+A\times 2+B\times 3}{A+B}
\]
Answer: 18.82 days

Q452. A job can be finished in 20 days. If work continues for 4 days, what fraction is still incomplete?
A. 1/5
B. 4/5
C. 4/1
D. 5/4

Family: tw_basic_residual_work

\[

\[

\]

\]
Answer: 4/5

Q453. A and B begin a job together. Their individual times are 24 days and 6 days. A leaves after 4 days. What is the total completion time?
A. 6 days
B. 7 days
C. 1 day
D. 5 days
Answer: 5 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_forward_leave
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
W=(r_A+r_B)t_1+r_Bt_2
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
W_1=(1+4)\times 4
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=4+\frac{4}{4}=5
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=4+\frac{W-W_1}{B}
\]
Answer: 5 days

Q454. 10 workers have done 60 files out of 360. To finish the rest in 10 days, how many additional workers are needed?
A. 20 workers
B. 18 workers
C. 16 workers
D. 24 workers
Answer: 20 workers
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_worker_added_to_meet_deadline
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
N_{extra}=\left\lceil\frac{R}{r\times t}\right\rceil-N_c
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
N_{extra}=\left\lceil\frac{300}{1\times 10}\right\rceil-10
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
N_{extra}=20
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
N_{extra}=N_r-N_c
\]
Answer: 20 workers

\]

Q461. A can complete a piece of work in 18 days and B can complete the same work in 6 days. In how many days will they complete it working together?
A. 24 days
B. 12 days
C. 6 days
D. 4 1/2 days
Answer: 4 1/2 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_lcm_hidden_total_work
Explanation:
Take the LCM as total work.
\[
W=LCM(18,6)=18
\]
Find each worker's one-period efficiency.
\[
A=18/18=1,\quad B=18/6=3
\]
Divide total work by the combined efficiency.
\[
T=\frac{18}{1+3}=4.5
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{18\times 6}{18+6}=4.5
\]
Answer: 4 1/2 days

\]

\[
T=t_1+\frac{W-W_1}{r_2}
\]
Answer: 7.45 days

Q484. 8 workers have done 48 files out of 240. To finish the rest in 10 days, how many additional workers are needed?
A. 8 workers
B. 18 workers
C. 12 workers
D. 15 workers
Answer: 12 workers
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_worker_added_to_meet_deadline
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
N_{extra}=\left\lceil\frac{R}{r\times t}\right\rceil-N_c
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
N_{extra}=\left\lceil\frac{192}{1\times 10}\right\rceil-8
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
N_{extra}=12
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
N_{extra}=N_r-N_c
\]
Answer: 12 workers

Q485. Starting with A, two workers alternate. A completes 8 records and B completes 5 records on their respective turns. What is the completion time in days for 120 records?

\[

\]

\[
T=18.38
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[

\]
Answer: 18.38 days

Q486. If 24 people can use a food stock for 36 days, how long will the stock last for 25 people?
A. 38.56 days
B. 37.56 days

Family: tw_food_resource_basic
Explanation:

\[
M_1D_1=M_2D_2
\]

\[

\]
Divide the remaining stock by the new daily use.
\[
D_2=34.56
\]

\[
D_2=\frac{24\times 36}{25}
\]
Answer: 34.56 days

Q489. A can complete a piece of work in 10 days and B can complete the same work in 16 days. In how many days will they complete it working together?
A. 26 days
B. 10 days
C. 13 days
D. 6.15 days
Answer: 6.15 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_lcm_hidden_total_work
Explanation:
Take the LCM as total work.
\[
W=LCM(10,16)=80
\]
Find each worker's one-period efficiency.
\[
A=80/10=8,\quad B=80/16=5
\]
Divide total work by the combined efficiency.
\[
T=\frac{80}{8+5}=6.15
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{10\times 16}{10+16}=6.15
\]
Answer: 6.15 days

Q490. A team completes 56 files per day for 2 days and then 221 files per day after a team change. How many days are required for 840 files?

\]

Q492. A job needs 20 days to finish. After 8 days of work, what part of the job is still left?
A. 3/5
B. 3/2
C. 5/3
D. 2/5
Answer: 3/5
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_remaining_work_fraction
Explanation:

\[
R=1-\frac{t}{T}
\]
Put the given values into that relation.
\[
R=1-\frac{8}{20}
\]
Solve for the asked value.
\[
R=\frac{12}{20}=3/5
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
R=\frac{20-8}{20}=3/5
\]
Answer: 3/5

Q494. A and B together can complete a work in 8 days. A alone can complete it in 20 days. In how many days will B alone complete it?
A. 12 days
B. 14 days
C. 13 1/3 days
D. 28 days
Answer: 13 1/3 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_individual_from_combined
Explanation:
Set up the rate relation for this case.
\[

\]
Put the given values into that relation.
\[
\frac{1}{B}=\frac{1}{8}-\frac{1}{20}
\]
Solve for the asked value.
\[
B=13\frac{1}{3}
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
B=\frac{8\times 20}{20-8}=13\frac{1}{3}
\]
Answer: 13 1/3 days

\]

\]

\]
Answer: 12.29 days

Q496. A team adds 12 metres to a project each day, but 5 metres are lost each day. What is the time for 180 metres?

Answer: 25.71 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_digging_filling_negative_work
Explanation:

\[

\]
Add the outputs of all active agents.
\[
T=\frac{180}{12-5}
\]
Simplify the total output.
\[
T=25.71
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{W}{r_n}
\]
Answer: 25.71 days

Q500. A can finish a work in 18 days and B in 6 days. They work together for 3 days, then B alone finishes it. How many days are taken altogether?
A. 3 3/4 days
B. 1/2 days
C. 8 days
D. 5 days
Answer: 5 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_forward_leave
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
W=(r_A+r_B)t_1+r_Bt_2
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
W_1=(2+6)\times 3
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=3+\frac{12}{6}=5
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=3+\frac{W-W_1}{B}
\]
Answer: 5 days
